<main class="plan-doc">
  <header class="plan-header">
    <h1>Wiring is asserted at creation — landed evidence</h1>
    <p class="plan-summary">The roadmap can only follow wires that were drawn. This landing
    makes drawing them a condition of creating a plan: one predicate, reached from the
    write boundary, the audit and the sprint view, refuses an implementable plan that
    waits on nothing, feeds nothing and gates nothing &mdash; unless the plan says in its
    own document why it genuinely stands alone.</p>
  </header>

  <section id="overview">
    <h2>Record scope</h2>
    <p>Two commits. The implementation and its tests; the skills; the measurement; the figure below.</p>
    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/unwired-plan-rule/decision-flow.png"
           alt="Decision flow: a document takes the rule only when it declares reckon-type=plan; terminal statuses and plans that declare depends_on, blocks, informs, a gate or a plan-standalone reason are silent; everything else yields a finding whose severity is taken from plan-modified.">
      <figcaption>One predicate, three readers. <code>unwired_plan_finding()</code> in
      <code>reckon/doccheck.py</code> holds the decision; the audit, the create boundary and
      the roadmap all call it, so a plan cannot be refused at one door and admitted at
      another.</figcaption>
    </figure>
  </section>

  <section id="s6-grammar">
    <h2>&sect;6 &mdash; the skills carry the relation grammar (partially landed)</h2>
    <p><strong>What this landing covers.</strong> The first clause of &sect;6's done-when.
    <code>skills/reckon-create/SKILL.md</code> now states the grammar its relationship
    fields accept, <code>[project:]slug[#section]</code>, with a worked example of a
    <em>section gate</em> &mdash; a gate whose <code>required_evidence</code> names one
    section of another plan (<code>nova:cut-cell-refactor#s3</code>) rather than the plan.
    The example is written against the defect it prevents: a plan-level
    <code>plan-depends-on</code> for a need that only one section has reports the whole
    plan blocked from its first section onward, which is false and misleads every reader
    that consumes the critical path.</p>
    <p><strong>What remains.</strong> &sect;6's other two clauses are untouched here and
    belong to a follow-on node: the audit warning for a plan-level <code>depends_on</code>
    that could have been a section ref, and the roadmap raw view reporting a section edge
    for the two nova gates authored on 2026-09-19. Neither was in this node's evidence
    fence, and neither is claimed.</p>
  </section>

  <section id="s7-refusal">
    <h2>&sect;7 &mdash; Wiring is asserted at creation</h2>
    <p>Commit <code>5ded914</code> (implementation and tests), <code>e87e5e3</code>
    (skills).</p>

    <h3>The rule, and where it is reached</h3>
    <p>One predicate, <code>unwired_plan_finding()</code>, decides. A document is held to
    the rule only when it declares <code>reckon-type=plan</code>; a plan whose
    <code>plan-status</code> is terminal is exempt; a plan that declares any of
    <code>plan-depends-on</code>, <code>plan-blocks</code>, <code>plan-informs</code>, a
    gate, or a non-empty <code>plan-standalone</code> reason is silent. Everything else
    yields the finding, whose severity is taken from <code>plan-modified</code>: a warning
    for a plan that predates the rule, an error for a plan authored after it.</p>
    <p>The three readers call the same function. The audit
    (<code>reckon/doccheck.py</code>) emits it as <code>unwired-plan</code>;
    <code>edit_plan(create=True)</code> (<code>reckon/mcp.py</code>) refuses with the same
    message and deletes the file it had begun to write, so a refused create leaves no
    trace; the sprint view (<code>reckon/roadmap.py</code>) folds it into
    <code>wiring_findings</code> at the same severity. A plan that declares
    <code>standalone</code> round-trips it through a read.</p>

    <h3>The positive control was exercised, not asserted</h3>
    <p><strong>The refusal, shown.</strong> A create with no wire and no declaration
    returns <code>{"ok": false, "error": "unwired_plan"}</code> and the message names the
    three questions; the target file does not exist afterwards. The test asserts both,
    because a refusal that leaves half a plan behind is not a refusal.</p>
    <p><strong>The measure.</strong> The gate
    <code>pytest tests/test_doccheck_unwired_plan.py tests/test_roadmap_unwired_plan.py
    tests/test_doccheck*.py tests/test_roadmap*.py tests/test_mcp_roadmap.py
    tests/test_review_roadmap.py</code> &mdash; <strong>133 passed in 14.50&nbsp;s</strong>,
    exit 0, zero added failures against the base revision. The four audit paths are covered
    as the fence required: wired plan silent, unwired new plan error, unwired old plan
    warning, standalone declaration silent. The roadmap test asserts its message is
    <em>equal</em> to the predicate's, so the two readers cannot drift into two spellings
    of one rule.</p>

    <h3>The measurement over nova, and a prediction that did not hold</h3>
    <p>The fence asked for a read-only audit of <code>/home/ITER/mcintos/Code/nova/docs</code>
    recording how many of its live plans the finding names, which &sect;7's text predicts as
    <em>nine of sixteen</em>. Measured 2026-09-19, read-only:</p>
    <table>
      <thead><tr><th>Quantity</th><th>Predicted</th><th>Measured</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>Live implementable plans in <code>docs/plans/</code> (non-terminal, not archived, <code>reckon-type=plan</code>)</td><td>16</td><td><strong>27</strong></td></tr>
        <tr><td>Named by <code>unwired-plan</code></td><td>9</td><td><strong>1</strong></td></tr>
        <tr><td>&mdash; of those, at warning severity</td><td>&mdash;</td><td>1</td></tr>
        <tr><td>&mdash; of those, at error severity</td><td>&mdash;</td><td>0</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>
    <p>The single plan named is <code>forward-profile-validator-set</code>
    (<code>plan-modified 2026-08-26</code>, draft, no wires, no gate, no declaration) &mdash;
    reported as a warning, which is the severity the rule prescribes for a plan predating
    it.</p>
    <p><strong>The population is plans-scoped, and the definition is stated here.</strong>
    The 27 counts only the live plans that sit under <code>docs/plans/</code>. A walk of the
    whole mounted docs tree returns <strong>29</strong> instead, because two live plans sit
    at the docs root rather than under <code>plans/</code>
    (<code>mast-catalog-gpu-solve.html</code> and
    <code>mast-nonaxisymmetric-physics.html</code>). Both numbers are correct walks of
    different sets; a reader reproducing 27 must scope the walk to <code>docs/plans/</code>,
    and one reproducing the docs-wide number gets 29. The plan text's own count and the
    audit's finding count both use the plans-scoped definition, so they are comparable; a
    docs-wide count is not.</p>
    <p><strong>Why the two numbers differ, and why the instrument is not the suspect.</strong>
    The prediction is stale rather than the reader being blind, and the evidence for that is
    a positive control: the reader <em>does</em> fire on the one plan that qualifies, at the
    prescribed severity, while staying silent on 26 that declare wires. The population also
    moved &mdash; nova carries 27 live plans today against the 16 the plan text counted when
    the estimate was written. The plausible reading is that the wiring the prediction
    complained about has largely been drawn since, not that nine wired plans are being
    passed over; but the honest statement of this measurement is the table above, not that
    conclusion. A reader who wants the conclusion verified should re-run the probe and
    inspect the wires on the 26 silent plans.</p>
    <p><strong>A caveat that cost a reading.</strong> The first run of the probe reported
    zero, and the cause was not the code: <code>/tmp/reckon/</code> is a ten-day-old copy of
    the package (mtime 2026-09-09), and a probe run as a <em>script</em> puts its own
    directory first on <code>sys.path</code> &mdash; so <code>import reckon.doccheck</code>
    resolved to that stale copy and the finding was absent from it. The same probe run
    through a stdin heredoc (working directory first) resolved to the worktree and reported
    one. Any measurement taken by running a script out of <code>/tmp</code> in this
    repository is measuring that copy. The scratch tree is not this node's to remove and was
    left in place.</p>

    <h3>The checklist moved into the author's path</h3>
    <p>A refusal that arrives after the plan is written is a tax on the author; the decision
    belongs where the plan is authored. <code>skills/reckon-create/SKILL.md</code> Step&nbsp;1.5
    and <code>skills/reckon-edit/SKILL.md</code> now carry the three questions as a table the
    author fills from the sprint's plan list &mdash; one row per candidate,
    <em>does my first section wait on it</em>, <em>does it consume my evidence</em>,
    <em>does one section of mine wait on one section of it</em> &mdash; and each answer maps
    to exactly one relation to write: <code>depends_on</code>, <code>blocks</code>, or a gate
    on the section. A no to all three is recorded in the document as a
    <code>plan-standalone</code> declaration carrying its reason, because a declaration a
    later reader can check against is the whole point of the escape hatch.</p>

    <h3>Follow-ons this landing creates</h3>
    <ul>
      <li><strong>The <code>standalone</code> meta is not registered in the round-trip
      tables.</strong> The plan-state scalars that <code>read_state</code>/<code>write_state</code>
      round-trip live in <code>reckon/_plan_html.py</code>, and the write-path allow-list in
      <code>reckon/_store.py</code>; neither file was inside this node's write scope. The
      declaration is therefore read and written by a shared reader in
      <code>reckon/_schema.py</code> plus an explicit meta write at the create boundary, which
      meets every surface the fence named but leaves the generic text-mode path unable to
      set the declaration. Registering <code>standalone</code> in both places is the
      follow-on, and it makes the text-mode route work as well as the op route.</li>
      <li><strong>&sect;6's remaining clauses</strong> &mdash; the audit warning for a
      section-shaped need wired as a plan-level <code>depends_on</code>, and section edges in
      the roadmap raw view &mdash; are not claimed here.</li>
    </ul>

    <h3>The review findings, closed</h3>
    <p>An independent review of this landing
    (<code>r-20260919T121016391583</code>, <code>83/100</code>) left four findings. All four
    are closed, on the change that closes them named beside each; the closures land in
    <code>2b04c779</code> (the roadmap scan), <code>35d2953b</code> (the write path and the
    skill) and <code>604026a6</code> (this record), against base
    <code>0bf94fd5</code>.</p>
    <table>
      <thead><tr><th>Finding</th><th>Closure</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr>
          <td>The wiring scan read each plan's standalone declaration through the registered
          mount rather than the checkout the rows were inventoried from, so a worktree-scoped
          roadmap judged one tree's plans against another tree's declarations.</td>
          <td><code>build_roadmap(..., docs_dir=...)</code> now names the inventoried checkout
          and passes it to the wiring scan, falling back to the registered mount only when
          none is supplied.</td>
        </tr>
        <tr>
          <td>No roadmap test exercised a plan that declares standalone, so the declaration
          branch was untested and the cross-tree read had nothing to catch it.</td>
          <td>Three roadmap tests added: a declaring plan is silent, and the cross-tree
          disagreement is asserted in both directions.</td>
        </tr>
        <tr>
          <td>The create skill spelled the declaration as a meta tag while only the state-mode
          op was accepted at the boundary, and <code>standalone</code> was absent from the
          round-trip tables and the write-path allow-list.</td>
          <td><code>standalone</code> registered in the plan-html scalars and the store's
          write-path allow-list; <code>reckon-create</code> now names the
          <code>{"op":"set","path":"standalone"}</code> route.</td>
        </tr>
        <tr>
          <td>The recorded population of 27 live implementable plans depended on a
          <code>plans/</code>-scoped walk the record did not state.</td>
          <td>The definition is stated above and in &sect;7: 27 under
          <code>docs/plans/</code>, 29 for a docs-wide walk. The finding also asked for
          it in the audit's own message; that half is not reachable here, because the
          wiring message is defined in <code>reckon/_schema.py</code> and the audit entry
          point in <code>reckon/cli.py</code> &mdash; both outside this node's write fence
          &mdash; and a project's counts cannot be written into reckon source at all
          without breaking the repo-agnostic rule. It is recorded as a follow-on.</td>
        </tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>

    <h3>The declaration now round-trips, shown by the write</h3>
    <p>The gap the third finding named was reproduced before it was repaired. At base
    <code>0bf94fd5</code>, driving the store's own write path directly:</p>
    <pre><code>&gt;&gt;&gt; store._apply_set({}, {"op": "set", "path": "standalone", "value": "one-file fix"}, False, [])
OpError: unsupported plan set path 'standalone'
&gt;&gt;&gt; _plan_html.read_state(html_with_plan_standalone_meta).get("standalone")
None</code></pre>
    <p>The MCP <code>_edit_plan</code> route masked this: it splits a <em>set standalone</em>
    op out of the batch and writes the meta directly before <code>apply_ops</code> ever sees
    it, which is why the create skill's own literal route appeared to work while the generic
    write path refused the same field. After registering <code>standalone</code> in the
    plan-html scalars and the store's write-path allow-list, the same call is accepted,
    <code>read_state</code> returns the reason, and the meta survives a state write; the test
    added for it enters through the store path rather than through the MCP masking route, and
    separately through the ordinary edit path that reads the reason back out of the file and
    out of <code>read_plan</code>.</p>
    <p>The cross-tree defect is shown by the same discipline &mdash; two docs trees, one
    mounted and one inventoried, carrying opposite declarations for the same slug, with only
    the <code>docs_dir</code> argument varying and the verdict following it. The
    mount-only fallback is asserted alongside, so the parameter is shown to be load-bearing
    rather than incidental.</p>
    <p><strong>Gate.</strong> <code>173 passed</code> over the two wiring test files plus
    <code>tests/test_roadmap.py</code>, <code>tests/test_store_concurrency.py</code>,
    <code>tests/test_edit_plan.py</code> and <code>tests/test_mcp_store.py</code>, against
    <code>168 passed</code> at base <code>0bf94fd5</code>. Both runs carry the same three
    <code>test_edit_plan.py</code> failures, which predate this node and are recorded as
    follow-ons rather than repaired here; the added-failure set is empty.</p>
  </section>

  <section id="typed-section-records">
    <h2>&sect;1 &mdash; Typed section records and byte-stable regeneration</h2>
    <p><strong>Local implementation, 2026-09-24.</strong> The section parser and typed
    model expose a <code>sections</code> list alongside <code>section_declarations</code>.
    Every opted-in record carries <code>id</code>, <code>effort_hours</code>,
    <code>capability</code>, <code>attempts</code>, <code>status</code> and
    <code>links</code>. The raw resource read exposes the same records. Positive
    quarter-hour effort, non-negative integer attempts, the existing capability enums,
    declaration-compatible status and the existing plan-reference grammar are checked
    before a record is returned.</p>
    <p>Commits: <code>2d5357fc4521a76e9ecd86b6a3d4c2c80fbf8aa6</code> introduces the
    contract tests; <code>739dc8c65bd01881b6c6014d88bba24e14fd2176</code> implements the
    parser, model and writer; <code>8cba99030e92b11135972422932a0eed5db61f56</code> preserves
    legacy structural wrappers and shares the capability schema. Source paths are
    <code>reckon/_plan_html.py</code>, <code>reckon/_schema.py</code>,
    <code>docs/_shared/plan.schema.json</code> and
    <code>tests/test_section_record_round_trips.py</code>. The JSON Schema is generated
    by the repository's <code>write_json_schema()</code> function.</p>
    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/section-contract-and-computed-impl/round-trip.svg"
           alt="Semantic HTML parses to a validated six-field record, which the writer regenerates without changing authored prose. Invalid metadata is refused, and removing regeneration breaks reconstruction.">
      <figcaption>The reconstruction assertion distinguishes a working writer from one
      that merely leaves existing markup untouched.</figcaption>
    </figure>
    <h3>The three-record fixture</h3>
    <table>
      <thead><tr><th>id</th><th>effort_hours</th><th>capability: class / reasoning / verification / risk</th><th>attempts</th><th>status</th><th>links</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>design</td><td>0.25</td><td>general / standard / strict / low</td><td>0</td><td>done</td><td>inputs, other:reference#method</td></tr>
        <tr><td>implementation</td><td>2.5</td><td>orchestrator / deep / strict / elevated</td><td>2</td><td>implementable</td><td>design#result</td></tr>
        <tr><td>evaluation</td><td>1.0</td><td>general / standard / standard / moderate</td><td>1</td><td>deferred</td><td>empty list</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>
    <p>Records may live on the heading or in an adjacent
    <code>&lt;section data-reckon="section" data-id="design" ...&gt;&lt;/section&gt;</code>.
    Bare structural wrappers with no record metadata retain their authored contents and
    return no record. A partial record still opts in and is refused when required fields
    are missing. Source-span replacement preserves heading text, attributes outside the
    record contract and prose; the section-declarations map remains independent and
    unchanged. A status disagreement with an existing declaration is refused.</p>
    <h3>Reproduction and controls</h3>
    <p>At base <code>c590aaaade26b590739c61e873ef7581746cf36c</code>, the initial 19 cases
    all failed: the three-record parse raised <code>KeyError: 'sections'</code>, and invalid
    risk and effort inputs produced <code>DID NOT RAISE &lt;class 'ValueError'&gt;</code>.
    After implementation, the actual refusal receipts name the fields:</p>
    <pre><code>sections.0.capability
  capability.requirements.risk: 'impossible' not in ['low', 'moderate', 'elevated', 'critical']
sections.0.effort_hours
  Input should be a multiple of 0.25; input_value=0.3</code></pre>
    <p>The 35 focused cases cover both carriers, all six fields, byte identity,
    reconstruction, field updates, partial conversion, legacy containers, metadata removal,
    required capability floors, invalid values, typed dumps, the generated schema and the
    raw read. Three additional compatibility checks pass for existing plan round trips,
    live-plan reads and the schema response's existing 24 KiB bound. The capability field
    references the shared definition with required section floors instead of publishing
    duplicate capability definitions.</p>
    <p><strong>Declared mutation, executed at the committed implementation head.</strong>
    Removing exactly the sections regeneration branch from <code>write_state</code>
    makes <code>test_section_record_round_trip_is_byte_stable</code> fail at
    <code>assert reconstructed == rendered</code>. The existing-markup round trip passes
    first, so the failed assertion specifically observes absent regeneration. The mutation
    is applied to the parsed module in an isolated test process; the committed source is
    unchanged. The red log begins with the declared mutation verbatim.</p>
    <h3>Selected gate and retained limits</h3>
    <table>
      <thead><tr><th>Revision</th><th>Passed</th><th>Failed</th><th>Skipped</th><th>Added failures</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>base c590aaaade26b590739c61e873ef7581746cf36c</td><td>2475</td><td>25</td><td>3</td><td>baseline</td></tr>
        <tr><td>implementation 8cba99030e92b11135972422932a0eed5db61f56</td><td>2511</td><td>25</td><td>3</td><td>0</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>
    <p><strong>Gate passes by delta:</strong> the same 25 failure IDs remain and no new
    failures were added. The 35 new contract cases all pass. The selected gate also
    collects mounted-document conformance cases, so its population can change while peer
    documents land; the final run collected one additional passing case beyond the 35
    new tests. The retained failures are in capability migration, crew promotion/recovery,
    receipt/routing records, two SPA source contracts and one existing external evidence
    citation. They remain visible in <code>failure-delta.json</code> and are follow-ons
    for their owners. They were neither disabled nor repaired in this node.</p>
    <p>All runs used the shared root virtualenv, <code>PYTHONPATH</code> naming this
    worktree, <code>TMPDIR=/tmp</code>, a unique pytest basetemp and a 45-second per-test
    bound on the existing compute allocation <code>1277272</code>. Selection is the 70
    existing test files importing either changed module or reading the published schema,
    plus the new section-record test file at the implementation head.</p>
    <p>Logs and the complete selected-file and failure-ID lists are retained under
    <code>/home/ITER/mcintos/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260924T211306627533-section-is-a-typed-record/</code>:
    <code>gate-targets.json</code>, <code>baseline.log</code>, <code>reproduction.log</code>,
    <code>after.log</code>, <code>compatibility.log</code>, <code>after-compatible.log</code>,
    <code>negative-control-compatible.log</code>, <code>parse-refusals.json</code> and
    <code>lint-delta.json</code>. The intermediate broader gate is preserved: it exposed
    the bare-wrapper compatibility problem and the schema-size bound, both repaired in
    the committed implementation.</p>
    <p><strong>Remaining work.</strong> Conversion of nova's hand-written section tables
    is a follow-on outside this node. The write-boundary append/refusal, computed impl,
    attempt ownership and routing changes remain with their respective sections. This
    worker leaves the driving followup, plan and section statuses, implementation fraction,
    version and modified date unchanged; the coordinator owns integration and closure.</p>
  </section>

  <section id="cohort-at-merged-head">
    <h2>The cohort gate re-run at the merged head of main</h2>
    <p><strong>Measured 2026-09-25, read-only.</strong> This node writes no code. It re-runs the
    cohort gate at the merged head of <code>origin/main</code> and compares the resulting

  <section id="typed-section-write-boundary">
    <h2>§2 — Typed section creation, with the wider gate retained as blocked</h2>
    <p><strong>Measured 2026-09-25.</strong> The implementation is committed locally in
    <code>ae15796309309739255391b206dbd1cf92ae4b5c</code>, following the contract-test
    commit <code>1a819a671286ce674f4e7eb32cc006dd7fe5d9d5</code>. Changed source and test
    paths are <code>reckon/_store.py</code> and
    <code>tests/test_section_contract_is_refused.py</code>. The existing
    <code>SectionRecord</code> schema and HTML record writer are reused without changes.
    This record does not declare the section closed: the wider gate has three added
    failures in concurrently owned tests.</p>
    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/section-contract-and-computed-impl/write-boundary.svg"
           alt="A section append validates its record before writing heading, body and metadata. Invalid records and new numbered text headings without records are refused with an example. Existing prose keeps its normal edit path.">
      <figcaption>The two paths covered here are state-mode append and text replacement.
      The separate legacy insert_section operation remains a follow-on.</figcaption>
    </figure>
    <h3>What the write boundary now does</h3>
    <p><code>append</code> with <code>target="sections"</code> accepts an item containing
    <code>id</code>, <code>title</code>, body HTML, <code>effort_hours</code>,
    <code>capability</code> and explicit <code>links</code> (an empty list is valid).
    It validates the existing quarter-hour effort, capability and reference contract,
    initializes tool-owned attempts to zero, preserves a predeclared section status or
    defaults it to implementable, and queues heading/prose insertion with the typed
    record for one atomic versioned write. An invalid item, duplicate identity, embedded
    heading or structured-state fragment is refused before persistence. A plan with no
    structured collections can append before its closing main element.</p>
    <p>A text edit compares actual numbered heading identities before and after replacement.
    A newly introduced <code>h2 id="sN"</code> without a typed record is refused. Partial
    record validation failures also carry a worked example. Text edits to existing
    unrecorded headings remain valid; escaped examples of headings and headings in research
    documents do not introduce plan work. Existing structured-state protections still apply.</p>
    <h3>Reproduction and refusal receipts</h3>
    <p>At base <code>0bbeb7ec5559e4a9e0aa1a1d96f4b2c735e927e9</code>, the initial 32-case
    reproduction reported <strong>30 failed, 2 passed</strong>. Valid appends failed with
    <code>unsupported plan append target 'sections'</code>; the new-heading text refusal
    assertion failed with <code>DID NOT RAISE ValueError</code>. Both legacy-edit controls
    passed, showing that the fixture could reach the working text-write path.</p>
    <p>The public edit_plan wrapper was exercised against an isolated document fixture at
    the committed implementation head. State-mode append returned <code>ok: true</code>,
    version 1, and a reread contained all six record fields. Text-mode insertion then
    returned <code>ok: false</code>, <code>error: text_edit_error</code>, with this detail:</p>
    <pre><code>new sections ['s3'] missing effort_hours and capability: no typed section record.
Use edit_plan mode=state. Example: {"op": "append", "target": "sections", "item": {
  "id": "s2", "title": "Implement and verify",
  "body": "&lt;p&gt;Describe the work and its acceptance check.&lt;/p&gt;",
  "effort_hours": 1.25,
  "capability": {"version": "1.0", "class": "general", "requirements": {
    "reasoning": "standard", "verification": "strict", "risk": "low"}},
  "links": []}}</code></pre>
    <p>The refused public edit leaves both state and version unchanged. The tests also
    parse the JSON example from each contract refusal and successfully submit it through
    the same store boundary; the example is executable evidence rather than a substring
    assertion. The final test file contains <strong>41 passing cases</strong>. The focused
    gate including the existing record round-trip and section-write files reports
    <strong>86 passed in 3.44 seconds</strong>. Intermediate focused logs retain two
    fixture corrections: adding the required plan title, and asserting section order without
    requiring an empty followup placeholder that the writer correctly removes.</p>
    <h3>The declared negative control</h3>
    <p>The exact declaration was executed at the committed implementation head:</p>
    <pre><code>remove the check that refuses a text-mode edit introducing a new h2 without a section record in reckon/_store.py; the text refusal case in tests/test_section_contract_is_refused.py must fail</code></pre>
    <p>The control removes exactly the new check from <code>_replace_authored_html</code>
    in an isolated Python process using its parsed syntax tree. The committed file is
    unchanged, and the mutated function is retained as an artifact. The designated test
    fails with <code>DID NOT RAISE ValueError</code>: <strong>1 failed in 0.43 seconds</strong>,
    exit 1. The red log begins with the declaration verbatim and prints the source module
    path, revision and working directory.</p>
    <h3>The required broader gate does not pass by delta</h3>

  <section id="insert-section-typed-record">
    <h2>The insert_section op writes the typed record — refusal receipts</h2>
    <p>Commit <code>e4be85c957eff10856da81c36e81c9a88aa84415</code> closes the route the
    record above named as a follow-on: <code>insert_section</code> queued an authored
    <code>&lt;h2&gt;</code> with no typed record, so a new section could be created without
    its effort and capability while the <code>append sections</code> target and the
    text-mode check both refused one. The op now validates the same
    <code>SectionRecord</code>, refuses before any bytes are written with the field named
    and the worked append example attached, and registers the record with the plan state so
    the heading reaches the file carrying its contract.</p>
    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/section-contract-and-computed-impl/creation-routes.svg"
           alt="Three routes — the state append sections target, the state insert_section op, and a text edit introducing a numbered heading — converge on one SectionRecord validation that either writes the heading with its record or refuses before any bytes are written, naming the field and showing a valid append; a text edit that introduces no new section stays on the ordinary prose path.">
      <figcaption>Every section-creation route reaches one validation. The op and the append
      target share <code>_new_section_record()</code>, so the two state routes cannot
      diverge.</figcaption>
    </figure>
    <h3>Refusal and write receipts</h3>
    <p>Eleven new cases in <code>tests/test_section_contract_is_refused.py</code> pass at the
    head through the same store boundary the append cases use: three parametrisations of the
    missing-field refusal (each asserting the field is named, the plan bytes are unchanged,
    and that the example the message carries can then be written), six off-enum cases
    (<code>effort_hours</code> 0, 0.3 and <code>"1.25"</code>; an unknown capability class;
    an unknown reasoning value; a malformed link), one write case asserting the heading, body,
    typed record, declaration and ordering, and one case refusing an authored
    <code>attempts</code> count. The in-scope file reads 52 passed against 41 at the base.</p>

    insert_section op in <code>reckon/_store.py</code>; the insert_section refusal case must

    block with a bare queue call. Under it the refusal cases fail with
    <code>Failed: DID NOT RAISE OpError</code> — 11 failed, 41 deselected — so the refusal
    cases are exercised by the removed check and not by something upstream. Full log:
    <code>negative-control.log</code> in the run directory, whose first line is the declared
    string verbatim; the mutated function is retained beside it as

  <section id="section-writes-cases-carry-their-record">
    <h2>The section-write cases carry the record they insert</h2>
    <p>Commit <code>98c976c5e1db9bde6e82fd47c49955ca31fd0e2f</code> repairs the six cases the
    record above reported. Once <code>insert_section</code> validates a
    <code>SectionRecord</code>, a case that inserted a section carrying no effort, capability
    or links reached the contract refusal in place of the check it names. Every insert in the
    file now submits its record through the same op, so the authored-fragment and stale-batch
    checks are reached rather than replaced.</p>
    <h3>The defect reproduced before the repair</h3>
    <p>At <code>3d7afe483b5943c616398c390d76b262339119f7</code>,
    <code>tests/test_edit_plan_section_writes.py</code> reads <strong>6 failed, 4 passed</strong>
    — the four <code>test_insert_section_refuses_unsafe_authored_fragments</code>
    parametrisations (<code>duplicate-id</code>, <code>nested-heading</code>,
    <code>structured-state</code>, <code>plan-metadata</code>), the structured-boundary case and
    the stale-merge case. Each receipt is the comparison the case makes against the refusal it
    received, for example <code>3 validation errors for SectionRecord … effort_hours /
    capability / links Field required</code> followed by the worked append example. Log:
    <code>repro-base.log</code>.</p>
    <h3>What each case now asserts</h3>
    <ul>
      <li>The three body-check parametrisations keep their exact pinned detail
      (<code>insert_section body must not contain another h2</code>, <code>… structured plan
      state</code>, <code>… plan metadata</code>), now with a valid record present.</li>
      <li><code>duplicate-id</code> is refused by the state schema instead:
      <code>error: schema_validation</code> with <code>duplicate section 's2'</code> in the
      details, asserted by substring because the payload embeds the pydantic version URL. The
      record joins <code>sections</code> before the authored fragment is inspected, so the
      duplicate is caught earlier than the heading scan; the plan bytes are unchanged either
      way.</li>
      <li>The structured-boundary case asserts the same insertion point as before (after the
      records that existed, before the gates region) plus that the new section's own
      <code>data-reckon="section"</code> record travels with its heading, and that
      <code>audit-doc</code> exits 0.</li>
      <li>The stale-batch case is refused whole by the comment-only merge — the batch now
      moves <code>sections</code>, so the two writers no longer agree on the document — and
      asserting the file is untouched; rebased onto the peer's revision the same batch lands
      at version 2 with both comments and the inserted section present. The queued insertion
      is not lost, it is refused rather than half-applied. The merge's own arms (a successful
      commute and a refusal on disagreement) stay covered by
      <code>tests/test_landing_append_is_commutative.py</code>.</li>
      <li>One new case pins the refusal of a record-less insert: the
      <code>effort_hours</code>, <code>capability</code> and <code>links</code> fields are
      named, the message carries the worked append example, and no byte is written.</li>
    </ul>
    <h3>The declared negative control</h3>
    <p>The control named in advance was: <em>remove the typed record from one rewritten
    insert_section case; it must fail with the section-contract refusal.</em> Applied to a
    scratch archive of the head tree (<code>/tmp/sw-nc</code>), the mutation strips the
    record from the unsafe-fragment cases' request; under it the <code>nested-heading</code>
    case fails on the comparison between its pinned detail and the refusal it receives, which
    is the contract refusal naming <code>effort_hours</code>, <code>capability</code> and
    <code>links</code> as missing. The committed test file is unchanged, and the mutated copy
    is retained beside the log. The mutation script asserts it applied before the run: the
    record keys are absent from the replacement request and the replaced call is gone.</p>
    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/section-contract-and-computed-impl/insert-op-stage-order.svg"
           alt="A state-mode insert_section request passes five stages in order — authored fields, the typed-record validation, the queued write effect, the file scan for unsafe authored fragments, and the atomic versioned write — each annotated with the case that holds it; a stale batch ends at the queue whole and lands once rebased.">
      <figcaption>The op's stage order, the case holding each stage, and the one path that ends
      early.</figcaption>
    </figure>
    <h3>The cohort gate</h3>
    <p>Both arms run the same 79-file cohort — the union of the 78 files the sibling
    <code>insert-section-carries-its-contract</code> node published as its gate targets and the 40 files
    this node derived by AST search for importers of the changed surface — as one
    <code>pytest -p no:cacheprovider -q -n 4 --timeout=90</code> run with a per-arm
    <code>--basetemp</code>, executed in place on the fleet allocation. Each log's first lines name its
    revision, tree and command, then print <code>reckon.__file__</code> and the measurement cwd, so the
    tree that actually served the run is checkable rather than assumed:
    <code>module.__file__</code> resolves inside <code>/tmp/s22-sw-base</code> for the base arm and inside
    this node's worktree for the head arm.</p>
    <table>
      <thead><tr><th>Arm</th><th>Revision</th><th>Result</th><th>Duration</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>base</td><td><code>adfc6715</code> (the branch point of <code>3d7afe48</code>)</td><td>5 failed, 2755 passed, 3 skipped</td><td>366 s</td></tr>

      </tbody>
    </table>
    <p><strong>Added failure ids at the head: none.</strong> The head set is a strict subset of the base
    set, so every failure the cohort shows at the head was already failing at the branch point. The four
    survivors — two <code>test_capability</code> legacy-tier cases, one <code>test_ledger</code> read-view

    arms and are outside this node's write scope.</p>
    <p>One base failure does not appear at the head:

  <section id="audit-section-contract-findings">
    <h2>The audit reports the gap, and the fleet keeps moving</h2>
    <p>Commit <code>c84c7323931581c6e9f74caaaeb908ecda2c04d0</code> adds two findings to
    <code>reckon/doccheck</code>, the module behind <code>reckon audit-doc</code>. An implementable
    section carrying no typed record is reported at <strong>error</strong> once the plan already holds
    a typed record to compare against, and at <strong>warning</strong> while the plan still predates
    the contract; a followup whose body enumerates two or more deliverables is reported at
    <strong>warning</strong> as section-sized work. Only <code>error</code> moves the exit code, so a
    repository that predates the contract audits clean while the conversion is in progress. The
    finding's worked append example is imported from the write boundary's own refusal builder and the
    tests assert it byte-for-byte, so the audit message and the refusal cannot drift apart.</p>
    <h3>Both defects reproduced before repair</h3>
    <p>A probe over one record-carrying plan and one partial record was run twice: at the dispatch base
    <code>c473be21</code> in a pinned scratch tree that carries <code>docs/</code> (several cohort
    tests read the live documents), and at the head. The module each arm measured is printed by the
    probe itself.</p>

  <section id="record-attaches-to-existing-heading">
    <h2>The typed record attaches to a heading the file already carries</h2>
    <p>Commit <code>25baddea8ad92a0f82b787851b4221201762559d</code>. A plan written before the
    contract carries its <code>&lt;h2 id&gt;</code> headings and no typed record, and the only
    state route that could give it one was <code>insert_section</code>, which authors a fresh
    heading — so the conversion of an existing plan rewrote the heading it already had. The
    <code>append</code> target <code>sections</code> now discriminates on the item: one carrying a
    <code>body</code> authors the heading and its prose exactly as before, and one carrying only
    the record writes that record beside the heading the file already holds and nothing else. The
    span written is one <code>data-reckon="section"</code> element on the line after the heading,
    which is the place the reader recovers it from.</p>
    <p>An id with no authored heading is refused at the write boundary naming that id and carrying
    the worked append example, so the caller is shown the create shape it needed rather than the
    splice's internal <em>expected exactly one matching h2</em>. A second record for an id that
    already carries one stays refused as a duplicate. The op vocabulary names
    <code>effort_hours</code>, <code>capability</code> and <code>links</code> as required on both
    state write routes.</p>
    <h3>The cohort and the declared negative control</h3>

  <section id="impl-computed-from-section-records">
    <h2>impl is computed from the section records</h2>
    <p>Commit <code>8e67f2bc</code>, against base <code>bcca11f0</code>. A plan that carries
    typed section records now reports <code>impl</code> as the effort of its done sections over
    that sum plus the predicted effort of every remaining implementable section. Deferred
    sections sit outside the denominator, and a plan with no record in scope keeps its authored
    figure rather than reporting a fixed zero. The reproduction is the dispatch's own fixture: one
    4&#8202;h done section and one 6&#8202;h implementable section while the stored
    <code>plan-impl</code> meta reads <code>0.9</code>. At base the meta round-trips and the read
    reads 0.9; at the head <code>read_state</code>, <code>parse_plan</code> and the
    <code>parse_meta</code> regex path all read <code>0.4</code>, and adding a third, 10&#8202;h
    implementable section moves all three to <code>0.2</code> with no write to the file at all —
    the stored meta still reads <code>0.9</code> on disk throughout, because a read derives and
    never stores.</p>
    <p>A state-mode <code>{"op":"set","path":"impl"}</code> on such a plan is refused with
    <code>op_error</code>: <em>impl is computed from the section records on this plan … It is not
    authorable — move a section's status instead.</em> The refusal is at the set path every state
    route funnels through, so it holds for the MCP edit surface as well as the CLI. The regex fast
    path carries the derivation too, because the roadmap and the discovery rows read plans without
    a parser: the fixture plan reports <code>progress_pct 40.0</code> in the roadmap's
    <code>immediate_roadmap</code> row where the stored meta would have said 90, and the parity
    assertion compares that figure against <code>read_plan</code>'s own rather than asserting about
    either surface alone.</p>
    <p>The writer needed one rule to stay honest. Regeneration writes the <code>plan-impl</code>
    meta only when neither the document nor the state carries a record, so a contract plan is
    never given a stored figure it would then have to keep in step, and the byte-stability contract
    on a record-carrying plan holds. A plan outside the contract is written exactly as before,
    which is what stops an editor's authored impl from being silently dropped on a plan that has no
    records to hold it.</p>
    <h3>The cohort and the declared negative control</h3>
    <table>
      <thead><tr><th>arm</th><th>revision</th><th>result</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>base, the 20 files the change reaches</td><td><code>bcca11f0</code></td><td>9 failed, 393 passed</td></tr>
        <tr><td>head, the same 20 files</td><td><code>ec53f8f6</code></td><td>1 failed, 401 passed</td></tr>
        <tr><td>head over the ids that pass at base</td><td><code>ec53f8f6</code></td><td><strong>393 passed, EXIT=0</strong></td></tr>
        <tr><td>head with the derivation disabled</td><td>scratch import of the head, mutated</td><td>4 failed, 3 passed</td></tr>
        <tr><td>the prose-refusal case alone, before the repair</td><td><code>4d7f15bb</code></td><td>1 failed, 6 passed</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>

    red on both sides, and two roadmap cases

    base arm is driven by a script that asserts which tree served <code>import&nbsp;reckon</code>

    <code>cd</code>-based base arm can silently run the head package; its log's first line names the
    scratch tree.</p>
    <h3>Artifacts and integrity</h3>
    <p>This node's logs live beside its manifest at
    <code>/home/ITER/mcintos/.config/reckon/crew/reports/reckon/s22-coord/impl-is-computed-from-section-records/logs/</code>

    <p><strong>Repair under review, commit <code>ec53f8f6</code>.</strong> Review found the regex
    fast path wider than the parsed read: a record element carrying authored prose —
    <code>&lt;section data-reckon="section" data-id="first" data-effort-hours="4"
    data-status="done"&gt;</code> with text inside it — was refused by <code>read_state</code> and
    accepted by the fast path, so <code>parse_meta</code> and the roadmap reported <code>1.0</code>
    for a plan whose parsed state raises. The repair removes the fast path's own record reading
    entirely: it detects that a document declares record metadata and hands the document to
    <code>read_state</code>, falling back to the authored value when the parser refuses, so a figure
    exists only where the parser produced one. The reproduction is pinned by
    <code>test_a_record_element_carrying_authored_prose_yields_no_computed_figure</code>, which fails
    against the head before the repair (<code>4d7f15bb</code>: 1 failed, 6 passed, the case's own
    assertion catching <code>impl_source: computed</code>) and passes at <code>ec53f8f6</code> and at
    the base. The stored-meta assertion in the 0.2 case now reads the file on disk rather than the
    in-memory fixture, so a read path that rewrote the stored meta would redden it. The parity case
    that confirms <code>read_plan</code> and the roadmap agree on the fixture is
    <code>test_read_plan_and_roadmap_report_the_same_computed_impl</code>: it compares
    <code>read_plan</code>'s raw impl against the roadmap row's <code>progress_pct</code>, so neither
    surface is asserted about alone.</p>
    <p><strong>Writer gate repaired at <code>d206de34</code>.</strong> Review found the writer's
    gate asking a text heuristic — a <code>data-reckon="section"</code> mention plus one record
    attribute name anywhere in the markup — rather than the parsed state. A state-mode
    <code>{"op":"set","path":"impl"}</code> on a plan that merely documents the syntax inside its
    own code examples was therefore a silent no-op: the call returned <code>{"ok": true, …}</code>
    with the version unchanged while the file stayed byte-unchanged, and this plan's own published
    HTML matched the heuristic — its only matches are the syntax quoted in its code examples. The
    gate now reads the state's typed records and, for a state naming none, the parsed document's
    own record selector, which is what the paragraph above this one meant by "carries a record"
    throughout, now settled by the parser rather than by a substring test. A documenting plan
    carries no record element, so its authored figure stays writable and readable; a plan holding
    records, or being given them, keeps its <code>plan-impl</code> meta untouched on either side of
    the write, so a derived figure is never stored back as the authored one and removing a plan's
    records stays byte-stable beyond the record spans. The document half of that rule is
    load-bearing: gating on the state's records alone reddened
    <code>test_removing_records_preserves_headings_and_prose</code>, which pins the byte stability.
    The reproduction is <code>test_a_plan_documenting_the_syntax_keeps_its_impl_writable</code> — it
    reads the plan's authored <code>0.6</code>, sets <code>0.7</code> through the state route, and
    reads the file back as <code>0.7</code>; at the pre-repair head <code>b3b7dc86</code> the same
    call reports success while the case's <code>written != html</code> assertion fails on the
    unchanged file, and the repaired head passes it.</p>

  <section id="attempts-raise-the-class">
    <h2>&sect;5 &mdash; A section's attempt count raises the class, and the payload names it</h2>
    <p><strong>Measured 2026-09-27.</strong> Commit
    <code>c23de2217efe30f9b7772b90862163f365710a48</code>. Flight config declares
    <code>capability_raise</code> (threshold 3, class orchestrator, reasoning deep, verification
    strict) in <code>reckon/schema/flight.yaml</code> and its shipped default in
    <code>reckon/schema/flight-defaults.yaml</code>; a role's <code>by_capability_class</code>
    overlay carries the backend each class reaches. The generated
    <code>reckon/_flight_schema.py</code> and <code>docs/_shared/flight.schema.json</code> come
    only from <code>scripts/regen_flight_schema.py</code>, never hand-edited: review found the
    committed copies carrying an overlay description the source had already replaced, so both
    were regenerated from the committed source and committed at <code>11d097c4</code>, where a
    fresh generator run reproduces them byte-for-byte (<code>git diff --exit-code</code> clean;
    <code>regen-idempotence.log</code>). <code>resolve_section_routing</code> in
    <code>reckon/crew/routing.py</code> reads
    the section's own typed record and returns the raise it applied (class, reasoning and
    verification, upward only), the count that caused it, and the backend the raised class resolves
    to. The raise fires on the record alone, so a fixture carries a seeded count: the writer that
    increments attempts belongs to another section.</p>
    <table>
      <thead><tr><th>Arm</th><th>Fixture record</th><th>Observed</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>raised</td><td>attempts 3; general / standard / standard</td><td>backend
        <code>raised-lane</code>, settings <code>effort: raised-effort</code>; capability
        orchestrator / deep / strict; summary carries the raise, the count 3 and the backend
        name</td></tr>
        <tr><td>unraised</td><td>the same record at attempts 2</td><td><code>section-lane</code>, no class overlay settings present; summary carries no raise, no class and no threshold digit</td></tr>
        <tr><td>above the rule</td><td>attempts 4; orchestrator / deep / strict</td><td>no move recorded, summary says <em>already at orchestrator</em> naming the count 4</td></tr>
        <tr><td>partially below</td><td>attempts 3; orchestrator / standard / standard</td><td>reasoning and verification move, the class does not; <code>raised-lane</code></td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>
    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/section-contract-and-computed-impl/attempts-raise-the-class.svg"
           alt="A plan section's record supplies attempts and the declared capability. At attempts 2 the node resolves at its declared class on the role's own backend and the summary names no raise. At attempts 3 the configured threshold raises the class to orchestrator with deep reasoning and strict verification, the class overlay selects the raised backend, and the summary names the rule and the count that caused it.">
      <figcaption>The attempt count picks the class, the class picks the lane, and the returned
      summary carries the count so the coordinator reads <em>third attempt, raised</em> rather
      than inferring it. The rule is upward only.</figcaption>
    </figure>
    <h3>The added file fails at the base, by assertion and by schema</h3>
    <p>At base <code>9945557624511d329211b5b573cdf77d38b02ac4</code> the new file reads
    <strong>6 failed</strong> in 0.74&nbsp;s (<code>measured-gate-base-new-file.log</code>). The
    failures are the substance, not collection: the shipped-defaults case fails
    <code>assert None == 3</code> on <code>rule.get("attempts_threshold")</code>, and the fixture
    arms fail with base pydantic refusing <code>roles.implement.by_capability_class</code> as an
    extra input. At the head the same file passes <strong>6 in 1.74&nbsp;s</strong>
    (<code>added-file-head.log</code>).</p>
    <h3>The declared negative control</h3>
    <p>The control named in advance was: <em>remove the raise rule; the attempts-3 arm must resolve
    unraised and its assertion fail</em>. Applied to an archive of <code>c23de221</code> with the
    <code>capability_raise</code> block removed from the shipped defaults (the test deliberately
    does not declare the threshold itself, so removing the shipped rule is the mutation), the
    attempts-3 record resolves <code>section-lane</code> and the case fails on
    <em>assert 'section-lane' == 'raised-lane'</em> — 1 failed in 0.51&nbsp;s. The log's first line
    is the declared string verbatim, and the removal step printed the rule it removed, so the
    mutation is witnessed rather than assumed. The control log is
    <code>negative-control.log</code> in the evidence log directory below.</p>
    <h3>The delta against base, and what it does not cover</h3>
    <p>The measured set is the 47 test files that import the changed modules, run as one
    invocation per revision with the schema generators on PATH; each arm's header names its host,
    revision, module and working directory (<code>measured-gate-head.log</code>,
    <code>measured-gate-base.log</code>). The head arm reads <strong>23 failed, 788 passed,
    1 skipped, 6 errors</strong> in 427.20&nbsp;s; the base arm over the same files reads
    <strong>23 failed, 782 passed, 1 skipped</strong> in 441.22&nbsp;s. Twenty-two ids fail at
    both revisions. The single head-only failure, the dispatch-watch case, re-run alone fails at
    the base as well (7.37&nbsp;s against 7.79&nbsp;s at the head) on a rendezvous timeout inside
    the test's own controlled sleeper, so it is not added by this change. The six head-only errors
    are <code>tests/test_split_existing_runs.py</code> fixtures that fingerprint the workstation's
    own crew tree while peer sessions were writing to it; re-run alone the same file passes 8/8 in
    8.89&nbsp;s. The one base-only id fails in the base arm's scratch archive because the archive
    carries no <code>.git</code>, so its in-tree <code>git status</code> exits 128; re-run at the
    head it passes in 3.00&nbsp;s. So no id failing at the head is added by this change, and the
    change's own addition is the added file: 6 failed at the base, 6 passed at the head. A
    residual is stated rather than hidden: the base arm runs from an archive without git metadata,
    so "fails at base" there is not by itself a claim about the base code.</p>
    <p>Lint is unchanged on the changed modules &mdash; 12 pre-existing findings in
    <code>reckon/crew/routing.py</code> and 7 in <code>reckon/flight.py</code> before and after,
    zero in the added test file, and the three hand-written staged Python files pass the
    formatter's check. The generated <code>reckon/_flight_schema.py</code> is never reformatted,
    because the byte-identity guard compares it with fresh generator output.</p>
    <h3>What this landing does not close</h3>
    <p>The live dispatch call sites still resolve without the class, because
    <code>reckon/crew/dispatch.py</code> is claimed by a concurrent node; the raise is reachable
    through <code>resolve_section_routing</code> and the wiring is a follow-on. The writer that
    increments a section's attempts is the sibling section's work; the fixtures here seed the count
    instead. Section closure, the driving followup and the merged-head verification remain with the
    coordinator.</p>
    <h3>Corrections 2026-09-27</h3>
    <p>Review found two defects in the first landing record. First, the committed generated
    artefacts carried an overlay description the source had already replaced &mdash; they were
    regenerated before that description was edited and committed after it, so the earlier
    "regenerated in the same commit" claim was wrong. They were regenerated from the committed

    byte-for-byte. Second, the delta was first evidenced by a head log that recorded an earlier
    uncommitted run; it is now evidenced by the matched pair of one-invocation runs at the
    committed head and at the base, cited above. The correction commits touch only the two
    generated artefacts and these two documents.</p>

## Implementable sections

### §4 — attempts is a tool-owned counter — **unmet**

Nothing writes the field. The schema declares it (`reckon/_schema.py:879`,
`attempts: int … "Tool-owned launch count"`), the record writer carries it through to the file
(`reckon/_plan_html.py:721`), and the §5 resolver reads it (`reckon/crew/routing.py:285`), but:

- `reckon/crew/dispatch.py` and `reckon/crew/promotion.py` contain **no** section-attempts code —
  the only `attempts` matches in either file are unrelated prose (`promotion.py:2854`,
  `promotion.py:3080`, `dispatch.py:6477`; reproduction at `logs/reproductions.log` lines 16-19);
- no dispatch increment, no resume distinction, no completion outcome recording;
- no display: `reckon/roadmap.py` never mentions attempts, and the SPA's only `attempts` render is
  the unrelated velocity column (`docs/ui/velocity.jsx:82`).

## 1. Checkout-scoped wiring scan — PASS

  declaration file under the handed `docs_dir`.
- `reckon/roadmap.py:327-346` `_unwired_plan_findings(project, plans, docs_dir=None)`
  consults `_load_mounts().get(project)` only under `if docs_dir is None` — the
  registered mount is the fallback, not the source. The call at `:365` hands the
  parameter through.
- `reckon/roadmap.py:1202` gives `build_roadmap` a `docs_dir` parameter and `:2043-2044`
  forwards it to the single wiring call, normalised with `Path(docs_dir)`; the review
  documents keep their own separately resolved path (`review_docs_dir`, `:2025-2029`),
  so the two concerns are not conflated.
- Tests exercised first-hand at the merged head, all PASSED
  (`gate-named-tests-verbose.log` lines 13-15):
  `tests/test_roadmap_unwired_plan.py:126-137` (a declaring plan is silent),
  `:140-167` (the declaration is found in the inventoried checkout, with a positive
  control asserting the mount does *not* carry it), `:170-190` (the opposite direction:
  the worktree flags a plan the mount silences). The two cross-tree tests are a genuine
  pair — one asserts silence from the worktree's file, the other a finding from its
  absence, against a mount holding the negated content.

## 2. Write-path registration of the standalone declaration — PASS

- `reckon/_plan_html.py:116-118`: `standalone` is in `_SCALARS`, so `read_state` maps
  `<meta name="plan-standalone">` into the state dict and `write_state` writes it back;
  `:151`: `plan-standalone` is in `_PLAN_ONLY_METAS`, stripped for non-plan artifacts.
- `reckon/_store.py:1227`: `"standalone"` is in `_PLAN_SET_TOP`, the allow-list whose
  head guard otherwise raises `OpError(f"unsupported plan set path {path!r}")`.
- Round trip pinned and PASSED (`gate-named-tests-verbose.log` lines 27-29):
  `tests/test_doccheck_unwired_plan.py::test_store_write_path_accepts_the_standalone_declaration`
  (through the store text route, i.e. without the MCP route's op-splitting),
  `::test_edit_sets_a_standalone_declaration_on_an_existing_plan` (ordinary edit path),
  and `::test_create_accepts_and_round_trips_a_standalone_declaration` (create path).
- The recorded base-state defect — a `set standalone` op refused at the boundary and
  `read_state(...).get("standalone")` returning `None` — is entailed by the base code:
  at `0bf94fd5` the store allow-list has no `standalone` entry and `_SCALARS` has no
  field, so the refusal is structural rather than incidental. The masking route the node
  names is real and pre-existing: `reckon/mcp.py` extracts a `set standalone` op from the
  batch and writes the meta directly before `apply_ops` sees it, which is why a
  create-and-edit through MCP could pass while the generic store path refused — and why
  the new store-route test is the one that pins the fix.

## §3 — impl computed from section records

**Verdict: not-started.** No code path derives `impl` from the typed section records; the number is still authored and clamped, and the promotion rule reads the authored value.

**Evidence (base `9fafad1f`):**

1. **No derivation exists.** Instrument: `git grep -nE "impl" <base> -- reckon/_plan_html.py | grep -i "section\|record\|effort"` returns only the two scalar round-trip conversions — `reckon/_plan_html.py:369` and `reckon/_plan_html.py:1248` (`elif field in ("impl", "effort_hours", "wall_clock_hours", "version"):`) — i.e. `impl` is transported, never derived. Elsewhere the surface reads the authored value directly: `reckon/roadmap.py:79` (`_progress(plan)` reads `plan.get("impl")`), `reckon/mcp_views.py:1788` (`_plan_effort` derives *spent hours through* the authored impl — the inverse direction of what §3 asks).
2. **`set impl` is not refused on a contract-carrying plan.** `reckon/_store.py:1898-1903`: `if head == "impl":` leads straight to `working["impl"] = max(0.0, min(1.0, f))  # clamp 0..1 (was reject)`. The `impl` branch at `reckon/_store.py:1896` admits the top-level key and never inspects `working["sections"]`.
3. **The tool's own route writes it directly.** `reckon/mcp.py:2247` (`def _set_impl(`) applies the value through `patch_plan(...)` with no contract check.
4. **The SPA renders the meta value.** `docs/ui/plan.jsx:587`, `docs/ui/graph.jsx:314`, `docs/ui/shell-titlebar.jsx:336`; no "computed" label and no calibration column anywhere under `docs/ui/`.
5. **Adjacent machinery still reads authored.** The promotion rule `reckon/crew/promotion.py:3743` (`_require_impl_moved`) compares `record["plan_impl_at_dispatch"]` against the authored value at completion — a rule whose correctness §3 exists to secure by computing the number instead.
6. **No test.** Nothing under `tests/` asserts a computed ratio; the instrument is the search in (1) plus the absence of any `tests/` reference to a derived-impl function (the only `impl` fixtures are authored values, e.g. `tests/test_promotion_requires_impl_move.py:142`).

**Proposed node N1 — see "Proposed nodes" below.**

---

## §5 — capability raise from section attempts

**Verdict: not-started.** Neither half exists: no rule raises a node's effective capability class from section attempts, and no summary names such a raise. The nearest machinery counts something else entirely.

**Evidence (base `9fafad1f`):**

1. **No attempt reference on the flight path.** Instrument: `git grep -in "attempt" <base> -- reckon/flight.py docs/_shared/flight.schema.json` returns no matches at base. The flight config schema carries no attempt key, so no rule can be configured.
2. **The nearest counter is a different kind.** `reckon/capabilities.py:582` resolves capability from a run-level redispatch lineage (`attempt_kind`/`attempt`) — a count of dispatches of a *run*, not of a section, and it never reads the plan's typed records. A reviewer grepping for `attempt` under `reckon/capabilities.py` has found the run lineage, not §5.
3. **False-positive hazard, named so a later reader is not misled:** `reckon/crew/dispatch.py:5001` discusses lane-change attempts in prose (placement/lane bookkeeping). That is not a section counter either.
4. **No test.** Nothing under `tests/` exercises a section-attempts capability raise (instrument as above: no flight-config attempt key to exercise).

§5 depends on §4's writer: until N2 lands, attempts is structurally always 0. N3 can be developed beside N1 with the record seeded in its fixture, but §5 must not be declared closed before N2 lands.

**Proposed node N3 — see "Proposed nodes" below.**

**Live defect (the reason the verdict is not `landed`):** the wiring scan resolves its tree from the registered mount whenever `docs_dir` is not threaded — `reckon/roadmap.py:1474` (`resolved_docs = (Path(docs_dir) if docs_dir is not None else _load_mounts().get(project))`) — and four production callers omit the argument: `reckon/mcp.py:3428`, `reckon/mcp.py:4434`, `reckon/serve.py:1712`, `reckon/flight.py:1514`. A plan on disk in one tree is scanned in another tree's copy, so a standalone declaration (or its absence) can be read from the wrong checkout. Reproduced below as followup F2.

## Proposed nodes

Each node: one goal, exclusive write paths, a done-when whose assertion fails at base, and a negative control.

### N1 — compute `impl` from the typed section records (closes §3)

- **Goal:** a plan that carries section contracts reports `impl` computed from those records, never the authored meta; a hand-set value is refused.
- **Write paths:** `reckon/_plan_html.py`, `reckon/_store.py`, `reckon/mcp.py`, `reckon/mcp_views.py`, `reckon/roadmap.py`, `docs/ui/plan.jsx`, `docs/ui/graph.jsx`, `docs/ui/shell-titlebar.jsx`, `tests/test_impl_is_computed.py`.
- **Done-when:** on a fixture plan whose records say one 4 h `done` section and one 6 h `implementable` section while the stored `plan-impl` meta is 0.9, the state read returns `impl == 0.4`; promoting a third 10 h `implementable` section moves it to 0.2 with no other write; a state-mode `{"op":"set","path":"impl"}` on that plan is refused with the computed source named; a plan with no section records still reads its authored value.
  - *Assertion failing at base:* the `impl == 0.4` read — the base round-trips the meta (`reckon/_plan_html.py:369`, `:1248`) and returns 0.9.
- **Negative control:** remove the derivation so the read falls back to the meta — the 0.4 assertion goes red. Secondary control: a record-less plan's authored value survives (guards against returning `None`/0 for plans outside the contract).

### N2 — a writer for section attempts (closes §4)

- **Goal:** `attempts` moves only through the tool — a dispatch increments it, a resume does not, and completion records the attempt's outcome on the record.
- **Write paths:** `reckon/crew/dispatch.py`, `reckon/crew/promotion.py`, `reckon/_store.py` (the record write path), `tests/test_section_attempts_counter.py`.
- **Done-when:** fixture plan with one typed record at attempts 0; one dispatch leaves it at 1; resuming that run leaves it at 1; a redispatch-and-promote cycle leaves it at 3 with the promotion outcome recorded on the record; the counter survives the plan's HTML round trip unchanged.
  - *Assertion failing at base:* after the full cycle the record's attempts is `3` — at base no writer exists and the field stays 0 (writer search over `reckon/crew/` returns no section-record reference).
- **Negative control:** delete the increment call — the count assertion goes red; make resume increment — the resume assertion goes red.

### N3 — capability raise from section attempts (closes §5)

- **Goal:** a node whose section record is at or above the configured attempts threshold resolves at the raised capability class and the dispatch names the raise; below the threshold nothing changes.
- **Write paths:** `reckon/crew/routing.py` (effective-class resolution), `reckon/flight.py`, `docs/_shared/flight.schema.json`, `tests/test_section_attempts_capability.py`.
- **Done-when:** with the flight rule at its default threshold, a dispatch on a fixture record carrying attempts 3 resolves to the raised class's backend and the dispatch payload names the raise and the count; the same fixture at attempts 2 resolves to the unraised backend and names nothing.
  - *Assertion failing at base:* the attempts-3 arm's raised resolution — no attempt key exists in the config or schema at base (`git grep -in "attempt" -- reckon/flight.py docs/_shared/flight.schema.json` is empty), so the fixture cannot even be configured.
- **Negative control:** remove the raise rule — the attempts-3 arm resolves unraised (red), while the attempts-2 arm asserts the raise did not over-fire. Note: this node can be developed beside N1 by seeding the record in its fixture, but §5 cannot be declared closed until N2 lands, because until then no real plan can carry attempts above 0.

### N4 — section-granularity in the audit and the critical path, and the frozen census figure (closes §6 clauses 2–3; folds in F3)

- **Goal:** where a plan's dependency could be a section ref, the audit says so, and a section-qualified dependency reaches the critical path as a section edge.
- **Write paths:** `reckon/doccheck.py`, `reckon/roadmap.py`, `tests/test_audit_section_refs.py`, `tests/test_critical_path_section_edges.py`.
- **Done-when:** (a) audit-doc over a fixture where plan A's plan-level `depends_on` names B and B's next implementable section is what A consumes reports one warn naming the section anchor; (b) `build_roadmap(...)["critical_path"]` over a graph where A depends_on `B#s2` carries the section anchor through to the path payload; (c) `reckon/doccheck.py:142`'s census figure is gone (mechanism stated instead).
  - *Assertions failing at base:* (a) no such finding is emitted — `reckon/doccheck.py:127-129` declares only `section-without-contract`, `followup-describes-section-work` and `section-record-invalid`; (b) `critical_path` carries bare plan slugs — `reckon/roadmap.py:1188`, `:1212`; (c) the literal count is present.
- **Negative control:** remove the warn rule — (a) goes silent; revert the qualified edge to plan-level — (b) loses the anchor; `grep -c "1 of 468" reckon/doccheck.py` returns 0 after the fix, non-zero before.

### N5 — the wiring scan reads each caller's own checkout (closes F2; carries F4's message half)

- **Goal:** every production reader of `build_roadmap` scans the caller's inventoried checkout, and the audit message states the plans-scoped population.
- **Write paths:** `reckon/mcp.py`, `reckon/serve.py`, `reckon/flight.py`, `reckon/_schema.py`, `reckon/cli.py`, `tests/test_wiring_scan_reads_the_inventoried_checkout.py`.
- **Done-when:** a test that drives a production caller (not `build_roadmap` directly — that path is already pinned at `tests/test_roadmap_unwired_plan.py:140`) against a worktree whose plan copy declares standalone while the registered mount's copy does not; the caller's scoped result carries no `unwired-plan` finding for that slug, and the inverse arm fires the finding; plus one assertion that the audit message names the plans-scoped population.
  - *Assertion failing at base:* the scoped caller result — at base the caller passes no `docs_dir`, `reckon/roadmap.py:1474` falls back to the registered mount, and the finding appears where it should not.
- **Negative control:** drop the pass-through in one caller — that caller's arm reports the finding again (red).

## Lanes — proposed nodes grouped by write-path disjointness

| Lane | Nodes (in order) | Principle | Runs |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | N1 | `reckon/_plan_html.py`, `reckon/_store.py`, `reckon/mcp.py`, `reckon/mcp_views.py`, `reckon/roadmap.py`, `docs/ui/*.jsx`, `tests/test_impl_is_computed.py` | immediately |
| **B** | N3 | `reckon/crew/routing.py`, `reckon/flight.py`, `docs/_shared/flight.schema.json`, `tests/test_section_attempts_capability.py` | immediately, parallel with A (disjoint paths; record seeded in its fixture) |
| **C** | N2 | `reckon/crew/dispatch.py`, `reckon/crew/promotion.py`, `reckon/_store.py`, `tests/test_section_attempts_counter.py` | **serially after A** — shares `reckon/_store.py` |
| **D** | N4 | `reckon/doccheck.py`, `reckon/roadmap.py`, `tests/test_audit_section_refs.py`, `tests/test_critical_path_section_edges.py` | **serially after A** — shares `reckon/roadmap.py`; C and D are disjoint and may run together |
| **E** | N5 | `reckon/mcp.py`, `reckon/serve.py`, `reckon/flight.py`, `reckon/_schema.py`, `reckon/cli.py`, `tests/test_wiring_scan_reads_the_inventoried_checkout.py` | **serially after A and B** — shares `reckon/mcp.py` with A and `reckon/flight.py` with B; disjoint from C and D, so it may run beside them |

**Serial set:** N2 and N4 after N1; N5 after N1 and N3. Every other pair is disjoint.

**Ordering that is semantic, not a file conflict:** N3's done-when can be developed in parallel with A by seeding the record in its fixture, but §5 must not be declared closed before N2 lands — the plan's own followup F1 orders "§4 and §5" after §3, and a raise on a counter that can never exceed 0 protects nothing.

unknown · undated

Coordinator ruling, 2026-10-04: the attempt count is derived, not stored. The first implementation (run attempts-is-a-tool-owned-counter, head da7ff47cd) wrote and committed the count into the plan HTML at dispatch. In this shared checkout that has five defects. A review node carries the reviewed section, so every review counted as an attempt. Dispatch refused whenever the plan file held an uncommitted edit, which routinely belongs to a coordinator or a peer. Two concurrent dispatches on one plan refused each other between one write and its commit. A commit inside dispatch fails during a peer's open merge or under a held index lock. Its review found a fifth: a failed commit left an uncommitted increment that blocks the next dispatch.

So a section's attempts is the number of distinct implement- or test-role runs naming that plan and section, across committed ledger rows and live pointers. Each counted run's outcome comes from its ledger row: promoted when the gate passed, failed with its classification, superseded otherwise. A live run reads as in flight. A resume or a lane-change redispatch keeps its run id and adds nothing. A redispatch under a new node id adds one. Reviews and investigations never count. The count is computed once in Python and delivered on the section record and on the roadmap's ready set. The SPA renders it. Nothing writes it, which keeps the section's rule that nothing else writes the counter and makes its round trip through edit and merge trivial. The run is sent back with this ruling.

unknown · undated

§4 landed on 2026-10-04, merged at 64d48f52f, under the coordinator's ruling that the count is derived.

  • The count. A section's attempts are the distinct implement- or test-role runs naming that plan and section, across committed ledger rows and live pointers. Each outcome is taken from its ledger row: promoted, failed with its classification, or superseded. A live run counts as in flight.
  • Where it shows. It is delivered on the section record in the MCP and server views and on the roadmap's ready set.
  • Routing. Section routing's raise rule now reads it, so the rule acts on real run history for the first time.
  • Storage and dispatch. Nothing writes the count. data-attempts round-trips byte for byte as a legacy attribute, the dirty-plan dispatch refusal is unchanged, and the store's write path does no ledger scan.

It took four rounds: a dispatch-time write, a relaxed visibility guard, routing and preservation regressions, and finally this. The last stacked whole suite at main 61abcb5df had 5 failures at base and 5 at head, flaky ids only, and every one of the 13 earlier failures passes. The focused gate on the merged code ran 84 passed. The final review scored 89.

Follow-on: the derivation costs the roadmap build about 8 s (29.9 s to 38.0 s) and the section view about 3 s on a 3604-row ledger, because it runs on every read. A cache keyed to the ledger's version and the live pointer set would remove that.

§13 landed on 2026-10-04, merged at f2cd3c6d4. The derived attempt aggregation is cached against the ledger index stamp, the newest run file and the live-pointer set's names, modification times and sizes, behind a lock. Unchanged inputs aggregate once across ten reads. Launch, promotion, discard, pointer replacement and newest-run replacement each refresh it.

Measured by the worker over this repository's real ledger of 1,820 run files: a repeated roadmap build took 3.03 s at base and 1.49 s at head, and a repeated section view 4.55 s and 3.50 s. The coordinator's stacked 43-file population at main 779dfae9f ran 635 passed at base and head. Review scored 94; its follow-ons are a richer index-stamp fixture and naming newest-by-run-id in the docstring.
